172: +// WithTopologyEnv sets the env vars (key=value entries) that are used instead of the
173: +// process environment to expand env vars in the topology and in startup config templates,
174: +// and that __IMPORT_ENVS imports. This keeps the environment of a program that embeds
175: +// containerlab, e.g. its secrets, out of the topologies it deploys.
176: +// It must precede the options that load the topology.
177: +func WithTopologyEnv(env []string) ClabOption {
178: +	return func(c *CLab) error {

180: +			return errors.New("topology env must be set before the topology is loaded")

184: +		c.topologyEnv = append(make([]string, 0, len(env)), env...)

186: +		return nil
187: +	}

611: +func SubstituteEnvs(b []byte, env []string) ([]byte, error) {
612: +	if env == nil {
613: +		env = os.Environ()
614: +	}
615: +
616: +	s, err := parse.New("bytes", env, &parse.Restrictions{NoDigit: true, NoReplace: true}).
617: +		Parse(string(b))
618: +	if err != nil {
619: +		return nil, err
620: +	}
621: +
622: +	return []byte(s), nil
623: +}

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.",

1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)

7: import (
8: 	"bytes"
9: 	"fmt"
10: 	"io/fs"
11: 	"os"
12: 	"path/filepath"
13: 	"slices"
14: 	"text/template"
15: 
16: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
17: 	clabtypes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/types"
18: 	clabutils "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/utils"
19: 	"gopkg.in/yaml.v2"
20: )
21: 
22: const (
23: 	varFileSuffix = "_vars"
24: )
25: 
26: // ExportRenderedTopology controls whether the rendered topology YAML is saved to disk.
27: var ExportRenderedTopology string
28: 
29: // LoadTopologyFromFile loads a topology by the topo file path
30: // parses the topology file into c.Conf structure
31: // as well as populates the TopoFile structure with the topology file related information.
32: func (c *CLab) LoadTopologyFromFile(topo string, varsFiles []string) error {
33: 	var err error
34: 
35: 	c.TopoPaths, err = clabtypes.NewTopoPaths(topo, varsFiles)
36: 	if err != nil {
37: 		return err
38: 	}
39: 
40: 	// load the topology file/template
41: 	topologyTemplate, err := template.New(c.TopoPaths.TopologyFilenameBase()).
42: 		Funcs(clabutils.CreateFuncs()).
43: 		ParseFiles(c.TopoPaths.TopologyFilenameAbsPath())
44: 	if err != nil {
45: 		return err
46: 	}
47: 
48: 	// if existing, load subtemplates that can be included in the topology file
49: 	fsys := os.DirFS(c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir())
50: 	subtemplates, err := fs.Glob(fsys, "clab_templates/*.gotmpl")
51: 	if err != nil {
52: 		return err
53: 	}
54: 	if len(subtemplates) != 0 {
55: 		log.Debugf("found %d subtemplates, parsing...", len(subtemplates))
56: 		upd, err := topologyTemplate.ParseFS(fsys, subtemplates...)
57: 		if err != nil {
58: 			return err
59: 		}
60: 		topologyTemplate = upd
61: 	}
62: 	log.Debugf("loading template variables...")
63: 
64: 	// read template variables
65: 	templateVars, err := readTemplateVariables(c.TopoPaths, varsFiles)
66: 	if err != nil {
67: 		return err
68: 	}
69: 
70: 	log.Debugf("template variables: %v", templateVars)
71: 
72: 	buf := new(bytes.Buffer)
73: 
74: 	err = topologyTemplate.Execute(buf, templateVars)
75: 	if err != nil {
76: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to execute template: %v", err)
77: 	}
78: 
79: 	log.Debugf("topology:\n%s\n", buf.String())
80: 
81: 	// expand env vars if any
82: 	// do not replace vars initialized with defaults
83: 	// and do not replace vars that are not set
84: 	yamlFile, err := clabutils.SubstituteEnvs(buf.Bytes(), c.topologyEnv)
85: 	if err != nil {
86: 		return err
87: 	}
88: 
89: 	c.renderedTopology = append(c.renderedTopology[:0], yamlFile...)
90: 
91: 	// save the rendered topology to disk if requested
92: 	if ExportRenderedTopology != "" {
93: 		if err := os.WriteFile(ExportRenderedTopology, yamlFile, 0o644); err != nil {
94: 			return fmt.Errorf(
95: 				"failed to save rendered topology to %s: %w",
96: 				ExportRenderedTopology,
97: 				err,
98: 			)
99: 		}
100: 		log.Infof("Rendered topology saved to %s", ExportRenderedTopology)
101: 	}
102: 
103: 	err = yaml.UnmarshalStrict(yamlFile, c.Config)
104: 	if err != nil {
105: 		return fmt.Errorf(
106: 			"%w\nConsult with release notes to see if any fields were changed/removed", err,
107: 		)
108: 	}
109: 
110: 	c.Config.Topology.ImportEnvsFrom(c.topologyEnv)
111: 
112: 	return nil
113: }
114: 
115: func mergeTemplateVariables(dst, src any) any {
116: 	dstMap, dstOK := dst.(map[string]any)
117: 	srcMap, srcOK := src.(map[string]any)
118: 
119: 	if !dstOK || !srcOK {
120: 		// For non-maps, src overrides dst
121: 		return src
122: 	}
123: 
124: 	for key, srcVal := range srcMap {
125: 		if dstVal, exists := dstMap[key]; exists {
126: 			dstMap[key] = mergeTemplateVariables(dstVal, srcVal)
127: 		} else {
128: 			dstMap[key] = srcVal
129: 		}
130: 	}
131: 
132: 	return dstMap
133: }
134: 
135: func findVarsFiles(paths *clabtypes.TopoPaths) ([]string, error) {
136: 	topo_dir := paths.TopologyFileDir()
137: 	vars_search_glob := fmt.Sprintf("%s%s.*", paths.TopologyFilenameWithoutExt(), varFileSuffix)
138: 	// e.g. lab_a.clab_vars.*
139: 
140: 	// this will find both lab_a.clab_vars.yml, as well as lab_a.clab_vars.additions.yml;
141: 	// their values will be merged in alphabetical order
142: 	fsys := os.DirFS(topo_dir)
143: 
144: 	valid_exts := []string{".yaml", ".yml", ".json"}
145: 
146: 	result := []string{}
147: 	candidates, err := fs.Glob(fsys, vars_search_glob)
148: 	if err != nil {
149: 		return nil, err
150: 	}
151: 	for _, candidate := range candidates {
152: 		candidate_ext := filepath.Ext(candidate)
153: 		if slices.Contains(valid_exts, candidate_ext) {
154: 			result = append(result, filepath.Join(topo_dir, candidate))
155: 		}
156: 	}
157: 
158: 	return result, nil
159: }
160: 
161: func readTemplateVariables(paths *clabtypes.TopoPaths, varsFiles []string) (any, error) {
162: 	if len(varsFiles) == 0 {
163: 		log.Debug("searching for template vars files")
164: 		foundFiles, err := findVarsFiles(paths)
165: 		if err != nil {
166: 			return nil, err
167: 		}
168: 
169: 		if len(foundFiles) == 0 {
170: 			// no var file found, assume the topology is not a template
171: 			// or a template that doesn't require external variables
172: 			return nil, nil
173: 		}
174: 		varsFiles = foundFiles
175: 	}
176: 
177: 	log.Debug("template vars", "files", varsFiles)
178: 
179: 	templateVars := make(map[string]any)
180: 	// read all requested var files, and merge their contents into one:
181: 	for _, varsFile := range varsFiles {
182: 		// skip empty vars file names
183: 		if len(varsFile) == 0 {
184: 			continue
185: 		}
186: 
187: 		data, err := os.ReadFile(varsFile)
188: 		if err != nil {
189: 			return nil, err
190: 		}
191: 
192: 		var parsedVars map[string]any
193: 		err = yaml.Unmarshal(data, &parsedVars)
194: 		if err != nil {
195: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("variables file '%s': %w", filepath.Base(varsFile), err)
196: 		}
197: 		mergeTemplateVariables(templateVars, parsedVars)
198: 	}
199: 
200: 	return templateVars, nil
201: }

5: package utils
6: 
7: import (
8: 	"fmt"
9: 	"os"
10: 	"reflect"
11: 	"regexp"
12: 	"strings"
13: 
14: 	"github.com/hellt/envsubst/parse"
15: 	"github.com/joho/godotenv"
16: )
17: 
18: // SubstituteEnvs expands env vars in b with the values of env (key=value entries).
19: // A nil env uses the process environment. Vars that are not set get their default value
20: // or are left unexpanded.
21: func SubstituteEnvs(b []byte, env []string) ([]byte, error) {
22: 	if env == nil {
23: 		env = os.Environ()
24: 	}
25: 
26: 	s, err := parse.New("bytes", env, &parse.Restrictions{NoDigit: true, NoReplace: true}).
27: 		Parse(string(b))
28: 	if err != nil {
29: 		return nil, err
30: 	}
31: 
32: 	return []byte(s), nil
33: }
34: 
35: // ConvertEnvs converts env variables passed as a map to a list of them.
36: func ConvertEnvs(m map[string]string) []string {
37: 	s := make([]string, 0, len(m))
38: 	for k, v := range m {
39: 		s = append(s, k+"="+v)
40: 	}
41: 	return s
42: }
43: 
44: func mapify(i any) (map[string]any, bool) {
45: 	value := reflect.ValueOf(i)
46: 	if value.Kind() == reflect.Map {
47: 		m := map[string]any{}
48: 		for _, k := range value.MapKeys() {
49: 			m[fmt.Sprintf("%v", k)] = value.MapIndex(k).Interface()
50: 		}
51: 		return m, true
52: 	}
53: 	return map[string]any{}, false
54: }
55: 
56: // MergeMaps merges all dictionaries and return a new dictionary
57: // recursively if matching keys are both dictionaries.
58: func MergeMaps(dicts ...map[string]any) map[string]any {
59: 	res := make(map[string]any)
60: 	for _, m := range dicts {
61: 		if m == nil {
62: 			continue
63: 		}
64: 		for k, v := range m {
65: 			vMap, vMapOk := mapify(v)
66: 			if v0, ok := res[k]; ok {
67: 				// Recursive merging if res[k] exists (and both are dicts)
68: 				t0, ok0 := mapify(v0)
69: 				if ok0 && vMapOk {
70: 					res[k] = MergeMaps(t0, vMap)
71: 					continue
72: 				}
73: 			}
74: 			if vMapOk {
75: 				res[k] = vMap
76: 			} else {
77: 				res[k] = v
78: 			}
79: 		}
80: 	}
81: 	return res
82: }
83: 
84: // MergeStringMaps merges all string maps and return a new map
85: // maps that are passed for merging will not be changed
86: // merging to empty maps return an empty map
87: // merging nils return nil.
88: func MergeStringMaps(maps ...map[string]string) map[string]string {
89: 	res := map[string]string{}
90: 
91: 	nonNilMapSeen := false // flag to monitor if a non nil map was passed
92: 	for _, m := range maps {
93: 		if m == nil {
94: 			continue
95: 		}
96: 
97: 		nonNilMapSeen = true
98: 
99: 		for k, v := range m {
100: 			res[k] = v
101: 		}
102: 	}
103: 
104: 	// return nil instead of an empty map if all maps were nil
105: 	if !nonNilMapSeen {
106: 		return nil
107: 	}
108: 
109: 	return res
110: }
111: 
112: // StringInSlice checks if a slice contains `val` string and returns slice index if true.
113: func StringInSlice(slice []string, val string) (int, bool) {
114: 	for i, item := range slice {
115: 		if item == val {
116: 			return i, true
117: 		}
118: 	}
119: 	return -1, false
120: }
121: 
122: // LoadEnvVarFiles load EnvVars from the given files, resolving relative paths.
123: func LoadEnvVarFiles(basefolder string, files []string) (map[string]string, error) {
124: 	resolvedPaths := []string{}
125: 	// resolve given paths, relative (to topology definition file)
126: 	for _, file := range files {
127: 		resolved := ResolvePath(file, basefolder)
128: 		if !FileExists(resolved) {
129: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf("env-file %s not found (path resolved to %s)", file, resolved)
130: 		}
131: 		resolvedPaths = append(resolvedPaths, resolved)
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	if len(resolvedPaths) == 0 {
135: 		return map[string]string{}, nil
136: 	}
137: 
138: 	result, err := godotenv.Read(resolvedPaths...)
139: 	if err != nil {
140: 		return nil, err
141: 	}
142: 	return result, nil
143: }
144: 
145: // MergeStringSlices merges string slices with duplicates removed.
146: func MergeStringSlices(ss ...[]string) []string {
147: 	res := make([]string, 0)
148: 	allNils := true // switch to track if all of the passed slices are nils
149: 	for _, s := range ss {
150: 		res = append(res, s...)
151: 		if s != nil {
152: 			allNils = false
153: 		}
154: 	}
155: 
156: 	// if all slices are nil, return nil instead of an empty slice
157: 	if allNils {
158: 		return nil
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	m := map[string]struct{}{}
162: 	uniques := make([]string, 0)
163: 	for _, val := range res {
164: 		if _, ok := m[val]; !ok {
165: 			m[val] = struct{}{}
166: 			uniques = append(uniques, val)
167: 		}
168: 	}
169: 
170: 	return uniques
171: }
172: 
173: // ExpandEnvVarsInStrSlice makes an in-place expansion of env vars in a slice of strings.
174: func ExpandEnvVarsInStrSlice(s []string) {
175: 	for i, e := range s {
176: 		s[i] = os.ExpandEnv(e)
177: 	}
178: }
179: 
180: // ToEnvKey capitalizes and removes special chars from a string to is used as an environment
181: // variable key.
182: func ToEnvKey(s string) string {
183: 	// match special chars to later replace with "_"
184: 	regreplace := regexp.MustCompile("[-+./]")
185: 	result := regreplace.ReplaceAllString(s, "_")
186: 	// match only valid env var chars
187: 	regAllowed := regexp.MustCompile("[^a-zA-Z0-9_]+")
188: 	result = regAllowed.ReplaceAllString(result, "")
189: 
190: 	return strings.ToUpper(result)
191: }
192: 
193: // NormalizeMapForJSON recursively converts map[any]any to map[string]any
194: // and []any elements to ensure JSON serialization compatibility.
195: // This is needed because yaml.v2 unmarshals nested maps as map[any]any
196: // which cannot be serialized to JSON.
197: func NormalizeMapForJSON(i any) any {
198: 	switch v := i.(type) {
199: 	case map[any]any:
200: 		// Convert map[any]any to map[string]any
201: 		result := make(map[string]any)
202: 		for key, val := range v {
203: 			var strKey string
204: 			if k, ok := key.(string); ok {
205: 				strKey = k
206: 			} else {
207: 				strKey = fmt.Sprint(key)
208: 			}
209: 
210: 			result[strKey] = NormalizeMapForJSON(val)
211: 		}
212: 		return result
213: 	case map[string]any:
214: 		// Recursively process existing map[string]any
215: 		result := make(map[string]any)
216: 		for key, val := range v {
217: 			result[key] = NormalizeMapForJSON(val)
218: 		}
219: 		return result
220: 	case []any:
221: 		// Recursively process slices
222: 		result := make([]any, len(v))
223: 		for i, val := range v {
224: 			result[i] = NormalizeMapForJSON(val)
225: 		}
226: 		return result
227: 	default:
228: 		// Return as-is for primitive types
229: 		return v
230: 	}
231: }

164: func WithRuntime(name string, rtconfig *clabruntime.RuntimeConfig) ClabOption {
165: 	return func(c *CLab) error {

175: 				// predict the lifecycle impact of link changes in their plan.

201: 			clabruntime.WithMgmtNet(c.Config.Mgmt),

205: 		}

208:

267: // WithTopologyVarsFiles records the topology template vars files on TopoPaths without loading a
268: // topology. Used when the CLI passes --vars without -t (e.g. destroy --all).
269: func WithTopologyVarsFiles(varsFiles []string) ClabOption {
270: 	return func(c *CLab) error {
271: 		if len(varsFiles) == 0 {
272: 			return nil
273: 		}

280: 		}

282: 	}

285: // WithTopologyEnv sets the env vars (key=value entries) that are used instead of the
286: // process environment to expand env vars in the topology and in startup config templates,
287: // and that __IMPORT_ENVS imports. This keeps the environment of a program that embeds
288: // containerlab, e.g. its secrets, out of the topologies it deploys.
289: // It must precede the options that load the topology.
290: func WithTopologyEnv(env []string) ClabOption {
291: 	return func(c *CLab) error {
292: 		if c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileIsSet() {
293: 			return errors.New("topology env must be set before the topology is loaded")
294: 		}
295: 
296: 		// copy into a non-nil slice, so that an empty env doesn't fall back to the process env
297: 		c.topologyEnv = append(make([]string, 0, len(env)), env...)
298: 
299: 		return nil
300: 	}
301: }
302: 
303: func WithTopoPath(path string, varsFiles []string) ClabOption {
304: 	return func(c *CLab) error {
305: 		file, err := c.ProcessTopoPath(path)
306: 		if err != nil {
307: 			return err
308: 		}
309: 
310: 		if err := c.LoadTopologyFromFile(file, varsFiles); err != nil {
311: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed to read topology file: %v", err)
312: 		}
313: 
314: 		return c.initMgmtNetwork()
315: 	}
316: }
317: 
318: // WithTopoBackup creates a backup of the topology file.
319: func WithTopoBackup(path string) ClabOption {
320: 	return func(c *CLab) error {
321: 		// create a backup file for the topology file
322: 		backupFPath := c.TopoPaths.TopologyBakFileAbsPath()
323: 
324: 		err := clabutils.CopyFile(
325: 			context.Background(),
326: 			path,
327: 			backupFPath,
328: 			clabconstants.PermissionsFileDefault,
329: 		)
330: 		if err != nil {
331: 			log.Warn("Could not create topology backup", "topology path", path,
332: 				"backup path", backupFPath, "error", err)
333: 		}
334: 
335: 		return nil
336: 	}
337: }
338: 
339: // WithTopologyFromLab loads the topology file path based on a running lab name.
340: // The lab name is used to look up the container labels of a running lab and
341: // derive the topology file location. It falls back to WithTopoPath once the
342: // topology path is discovered. varsFile is passed through to WithTopoPath when the
343: // topology file exists (same as global --vars).
344: func WithTopologyFromLab(labName string, varsFiles []string) ClabOption {
345: 	return func(c *CLab) error {
346: 		if labName == "" {
347: 			return fmt.Errorf("lab name is required to derive topology path")
348: 		}
349: 
350: 		ctx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), topoFromLabListTimeout)
351: 		defer cancel()
352: 
353: 		filter := []*clabtypes.GenericFilter{
354: 			{
355: 				FilterType: "label",
356: 				Field:      clabconstants.Containerlab,
357: 				Operator:   "=",
358: 				Match:      labName,
359: 			},
360: 		}
361: 
362: 		containers, err := c.globalRuntime().ListContainers(ctx, filter)
363: 		if err != nil {
364: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed to list containers for lab '%s': %w", labName, err)
365: 		}
366: 
367: 		if len(containers) == 0 {
368: 			return fmt.Errorf("lab '%s' not found - no running containers", labName)
369: 		}
370: 
371: 		topoFile := containers[0].Labels[clabconstants.TopoFile]
372: 		if topoFile == "" {
373: 			return fmt.Errorf("could not determine topology file from container labels")
374: 		}
375: 
376: 		// If topology file doesn't exist, fall back to using just the lab name.
377: 		// This allows operations like destroy to work even when the topo file is missing.
378: 		if !clabutils.FileOrDirExists(topoFile) {
379: 			log.Debugf(
380: 				"topology file %s not found for lab %s, using lab name only",
381: 				topoFile,
382: 				labName,
383: 			)
384: 			return WithLabNameOnly(labName)(c)
385: 		}
386: 
387: 		log.Debugf("found topology file for lab %s: %s", labName, topoFile)
388: 
389: 		return WithTopoPath(topoFile, varsFiles)(c)
390: 	}
391: }
392: 
393: // WithLabNameOnly sets the lab name without requiring or loading a topology file.
394: // This is useful for destroy operations where we only need the lab name to find
395: // and remove containers, without needing the original topology file.
396: func WithLabNameOnly(labName string) ClabOption {
397: 	return func(c *CLab) error {
398: 		if labName == "" {
399: 			return fmt.Errorf("lab name is required")
400: 		}
401: 
402: 		c.Config.Name = labName
403: 
404: 		log.Debugf("set lab name to %s (without topology file)", labName)
405: 
406: 		// Initialize management network with defaults so destroy can work
407: 		return c.initMgmtNetwork()
408: 	}
409: }
410: 
411: // WithNodeFilter option sets a filter for nodes to be deployed.
412: // A filter is a list of node names to be deployed,
413: // names are provided exactly as they are listed in the topology file.
414: // Since this is altering the clab.config.Topology.[Nodes,Links] it must only
415: // be called after WithTopoFile.
416: func WithNodeFilter(nodeFilter []string) ClabOption {
417: 	return func(c *CLab) error {
418: 		return c.filterClabNodes(nodeFilter)
419: 	}
420: }

package parse

type Restrictions struct {

	NoDigit bool
}

func New(s string, m map[string]string, r *Restrictions) *Parser {

}

No vendor dir. So I cannot inspect hellt/envsubst v0.2.0's `parse` package. I'll note this in coverage notes as a limitation: the semantic equivalence between `envsubst.BytesRestrictedNoReplace(b, false, false, true, true)` and `parse.New("bytes", env, &parse.Restrictions{NoDigit: true, NoReplace: true}).Parse(...)` could not be verified from the snapshot; in particular whether the old `noempty` flag (third bool = true) is preserved, since the new Restrictions doesn't set a `NoEmpty`-like field.

Hmm wait, actually — let me reconsider the old call. `envsubst.BytesRestrictedNoReplace(buf.Bytes(), false, false, true, true)`. Let me check the a8m/envsubst root API: 

```go
func Restricted(b []byte, restricted bool, noempty bool, nodigit bool) ([]byte, error)
```

hellt fork added NoReplace variant: `func BytesRestrictedNoReplace(b []byte, restricted, onlyknown, noempty, nodigit bool) ([]byte, error)`. The four bools (false, false, true, true) = restricted=false, onlyknown=false, noempty=true, nodigit=true. So old behavior: noempty=true (empty-set vars not replaced), nodigit=true.

New: Restrictions{NoDigit: true, NoReplace: true} — no NoEmpty. So IF the Restrictions struct has NoEmpty and it defaults false, empty-set vars now get replaced with empty string. This is a real potential behavior regression, but uncertain. Actually wait — maybe in hellt's fork, "NoReplace" IS the combination that covers empty + unset (i.e., the fork's NoReplace means "don't replace vars that are unset or empty"), and `BytesRestrictedNoReplace(b, restricted, onlyknown, noempty, nodigit)` internally sets NoReplace=true AND passes noempty. In that case dropping noempty=true is a regression.

Given tests: the topology env test uses `EXPANDED: ${GIVEN}-${CLAB_TEST_SECRET:=unset}` with empty env and expects `$GIVEN-unset`. Neither case involves an empty-set var. So this edge is untested.

I'll report it as P3 with honest framing: potential behavior change for variables set to an empty value, cannot be confirmed from the snapshot; recommend a test or verifying the restriction mapping. Actually, let me check the git history — is there any docs in the snapshot describing envsubst behavior? The docs table in topo-def-file.md:

| `${var:=$DEFAULT}` | If var not set or is empty, evaluate expression as $DEFAULT |
| `${var+$OTHER}` | If var set, evaluate expression as $OTHER, otherwise as empty string |

Hmm interesting — `${var+$OTHER}`: "If var set, evaluate as $OTHER, otherwise empty". In shell, `${var+$OTHER}` when var set-but-empty yields $OTHER. OK.
